Cythera is a Role-Playing Game, released by Ambrosia Software in 1999.

One sleepless night, the player is transported to the land of Cythera from their home by Alaric the LandKing. Alaric is bonded the land and has used his power to keep the land prosperous, but lately the bond is weakening, causing chaos across land. Plauge, violence, and treachery now threaten the land, and in desperate attempt to save Cythera and himself, he has used most of his remaining power to summon a hero from Earth to save the land.
To complicate things further, the hero is warned through a mysterious entity called Omen that Alaric may not be all that he seems and must be destroyed to return balance to the land.
And so with only a few leads, the hero sets off to discover the truth behind, well, basically everything. While Cythera is an RPG, the focus is less about the combat and more about figuring things out. The dialogue system allows the player to type in keywords that the NPCs react to, so it's important to figure out who to talk to and what about.


The below is the complete character list for Cythera.

Location - Land King Hall
Alaric (LandKing) - Land king of Cythera; son of Bahoudin/Magpie and Chrysothemis. Can teach Body, Mind, and Reflex. Summoned the player to help save Cythera from chaos. Gives the player an amulet and explains his weakening connection to the land. The ending varies based on whether the player uses a corrupted or purified crolna.
Emesa (Peasant, Female) - Cook in Land King Hall; wife of Hadrian and mother of Hector. Can teach Cooking. Can teach bread-making and shares concerns about her husband's dedication to protecting the ailing Alaric.
Guards (Guard) - Alaric's guards. Provide basic security and directions within Land King Hall
Hadrian (Guard) - Captain of the Guard; son of Lycus and Andra, husband of Emesa, and father of Hector. Can teach Attack, Defense, Sword, and Shield. Offers combat training and must give permission for his son Hector to join the player's party
Hector (Fighter) - Brave warrior; son of Hadrian and Emesa. Can join you on your quest. Young warrior who can join the player's party with his father's permission. Honorable and eager to prove himself. Provides information about LandKing Hall and discusses recent earthquakes and his training.
Magpie (Fool) - (AKA Bahoudin) Jester of Alaric's court; son of Jhiaxus and Jinrai, husband of Chrysothemis, and father of Alaric. Speaks cryptically and serves as an advisor. Revealed to be Bahoudin, son of Jhiaxus, and possesses a key to the Right Pillar.

Location - Odemia
Ake (Noble, Female) - Weaver; wife of Milcom and mother of Sardis. Can teach Weaving. Secretly shares information about House Comana's involvement in Ariadne's kidnapping if met in her garden at night.
Antiphus (Brigand) - One of Ariadne's kidnappers. Is hostile toward the player for interfering.
Ariadne (Noble, Female) - Noblewoman in House Nicander; wife of Philinus and mother of Ascalon and Opheltius. Kidnapping victim who asks to be rescued and returned to Odemia.
Ascalon (Noble, Male) - Nobleman in House Nicander; Son of Philinus and Ariadne and younger brother of Opheltius. His reaction depends on whether Ariadne was saved.
Crito (Merchant) - Owner of Titan's Head; member of House Dodona. Manages rooms, meals, and a dice game. Has connections to other tavern owners and seeks help regarding his feelings for Hebe. Initially confrontational if Aethon is present due to unpaid debt.
Gate Guard (Guard) - Guard watching over Odemia's main gate. Protects Odemia during Ariadne's kidnapping crisis, only allowing entry after her rescue.
Guards (Guard) - Odemia's guards
Hebe (Peasant, Female) - Threadmaker; engaged to marry Antenor. Quiet spinner who sells thread to Ake. Has unresolved feelings for Antenor and asks players to tell him she forgives him. Buys flax for 10 oboloi per bale.
Milcom (Miner) - Former elder of House Atussa; husband of Ake and father of Sardis. Now working as a blacksmith. Sells weapons and armor. Mentions his son Sardis is in plague-stricken Catamarca.
Peirithous (Merchant) - Majordomo of Judge Sacas; not in the final game, class of merchant is therefore a guess based on other majordomos. Provides basic information about Cythera's geography and politics. Knows details about major cities and ruling houses.
Philinus (Noble, Male) - Elder of House Nicander; husband of Ariadne and father of Ascalon and Opheltius. Head of House Nicander and leader of Odemia. His wife Ariadne was kidnapped but can be rescued by the player.
Sacas (Mage, Female) - Odemia's Judge. Investigates Ariadne's kidnapping. Asks the player to interrogate a prisoner immune to her truth spells and investigates the mysterious substance "kesh."
Thersites (Fighter) - Captain of Odemia's guard. Can teach Axe and Mace. Honorable Captain. Lost a family ring with inscription "To Hapede with Love" that can be returned to him. Concerned about Antiphus' mysterious drowning death in jail.
Tlepolemus (Hunter) - Hunter and fisherman. Can teach Fishing

Location - Catamarca
Darius (Fighter) - Representative of House Comana to Catamarca; youngest of the three Comana brothers. Appears deceptive when questioned about certain topics (with Ascertainment). Speaks critically of other Houses and claims House Comana has improved Kosha.
Eumelus (Drunk) - Drunk
Guards (Guard) - Catamarca's guards. Similar to Cademia Guards but stationed in Catamarca. Provide local information and directions while maintaining professional distance.
Mantinea (Noble, Female) - Noblewoman in House Strymon; wife of Propontis. Helps rule Catamarca. Distrusts House Comana and particularly Darius. Mentions seeing Darius meeting with suspicious company at night. Lost servants Andromache and Hapmonides to the plague.
Metopes (Mage, Male) - Catamarca's Judge. Initially concerned about a plague affecting the city. After the plague is cured, discusses a strange green crystal and polyp creatures found in underground springs. Explains duties of Judges including settling disputes and running Council meetings.
Parium (Merchant) - Runs the Green Goat inn; elder of House Dodona
Polydamas (Peasant, Male) - Majordomo at the citadel in Catamarca. Elderly majordomo who struggles with the plague initially. Serves as cook and doorman, and possesses a sapphire book left by a mage. Provides directions and information about the city.
Propontis (Noble, Male) - Elder of House Strymon; husband of Mantinea and older brother of Halos. Initially sick with plague but recovers. Discusses political matters, particularly tensions with House Comana, and is grateful to the player for curing the plague.
Sardis (Merchant) - Representative of House Atussa to Catamarca; son of Milcom and Ake. Actually works more for House Strymon than House Atussa.

Location - Cademia
Aethon (Peasant, Male) - Member of the Ratcatcher's Guild. Can teach Missile and join you on your quest. Knowledgeable about House politics and becomes available as a companion after meeting Halos.
Alastor (Miner) - Leather worker. Offers to make special boots from a gator carcass the player can bring him.
Alcestris (Wench) - Cook at the Tyrant's Castle. Elderly cook who shares gossip about various noble houses.
Anisa (Mage, Female) - Master historian. Can provide extensive historical information about Cythera. Knows details about the Houses, cities, and important historical events like the exile of Mages. Can help locate the Sapphire Books in exchange for learning about Alaric's mother.
Antenor (Peasant, Male) - Merchant of household goods; fiance of Hebe. Has a building for sale for 10,000 oboloi. Previously engaged to Hebe.
Apis (Wench) - Runs the Two Taled Rat; member of House Dodona. Offers food, drinks, rooms and dice games. Needs help getting flour from Periphas and offers a wine contract opportunity.
Asius (Merchant) - Majordomo at Tyrant's Castle. Judge Berossus' majordomo in Cademia.
Atymnius (Miner) - Miner previously working for House Attis. Had a traumatic experience in the Attis iron mine. Tells a story about encountering a glowing ghostly figure speaking an unknown language after a cave-in. Was too disturbed to return to mining afterward.
Autonous (Miner) - Official minter. Has been making oboloi (coins) for 12 years and expects to continue. Very brief in his responses.
Berossus (Mage, Male) - Cademia's Judge. Uses magic to detect truth. Involved in investigating a murder where Halos allegedly killed Opheltius but claims innocence. Later discovers evidence of a shapeshifter being involved.
Bias (Peasant, Male) - Servant of House Attis; husband of Thetis. Loyal to his house and disdainful of House Comana. Views Malis as like a son.
Bryaxis (Mage, Male) - Master, studying the connection between music and magic. Created special locks opened by playing specific tunes. Friends with the bard Demodocus.
Cybele (Noble, Female) - Noblewoman in House Attis; daughter of Thuria and sister of Malis. Beautiful young woman who becomes visibly strained after a murder. Provides political insights about house relations and is connected to the murder investigation.
Dares (Merchant) - Runs Dares Eatery; member of House Dodona and husband of Diomede. Runs an eatery with his wife. They serve meals, drinks and food items. Through drink conversations, they share various rumors about ghosts, House politics, and troubles in the land.
Deiphobus (Brigand) - Ruffian working for House Comana
Diomede (Wench) - Helps run Dares Eatery; wife of Dares. Her dialogue largely mirrors her husband's regarding food, drinks and local knowledge.
Dryas (Child) - Child living in the sewers as a member of the Ratcatcher's Guild. Witnesses the murder of Opheltius and can testify about seeing a shapeshifter. Hides in the sewers until someone can safely escort him to Judge Berossus.
Dymas (Child) - Son of Thoas the blacksmith. Mentions having a dream about the player character and a shapeshifter.
Eteocles (Merchant) - Head of Ratcatcher's Guild. Can teach Lock Picking. Offers membership for 50 oboloi (can be haggled down to 35), providing access to training in skills like Lock Picking and Thievery, plus the ability to buy special items.
Guards (Guard) - Berossus' guards. Professional guards who give basic directions and information about the city. They discuss local affairs and respond formally, avoiding sensitive topics.
Halos (Fighter) - Representative of House Strymon to Cademia; younger brother of Propontis. Initially busy and dismissive unless persuaded. After discussing Comana, becomes more friendly and talks politics. If jailed, desperately claims innocence. Provides information about House politics, the kidnapping of Ariadne, and his own murder accusation.
Hypsenor (Merchant) - Tailor. Reserved tailor who sells cloaks and capes. Gets fabric locally and from Ake in Odemia.
Ilus (Merchant) - Fruit-seller. Hesitant vendor who only speaks during business hours to sell grapes and pomegranates.
Laomedon (Hunter) - Meat-seller
Malis (Noble, Male) - Nobleman in House Attis; son of Thuria and brother of Cybele. Nervous man learning the family business and will eventually take over House Attis. Shows concern for his sister Cybele and expresses distrust of Halos. Provides directions around Cademia and information about various houses and locations.
Naxos (Peasant, Male) - Representative of House Comana to Cademia; middle of the three Comana brothers. Claims to have witnessed Halos commit murder, but Ascertainment reveals he's lying. Involved in the wine trade.
Neoptolemus (Drunk) - Retired merchant. Can teach Haggling and Persuasion
Oeneus (Hunter) - Bowyer
Opheltius (Noble, Male) - Representative of House Nicander to Cademia; son of Philinus and Ariadne and older brother of Ascalon
Periphas (Merchant) - Baker. Sells bread for 3 oboloi and provides flour to Apis.
Stentor (Merchant) - Former majordomo of Itanos. Former majordomo from Kosha who now does odd jobs in Cademia. Claims to have witnessed his friend Pelagon die by Scylla but then saw him alive later, causing him to flee Kosha in fear. After Opheltius' murder, claims to have seen Pelagon in Cademia. Has a lost net that can be returned to him.
Theano (Wench) - Cheese-seller
Thetis (Wench) - Cook and maid for House Attis; wife of Bias. Married Bias while working for House Attis. Distrusts House Comana and Halos. Loyal to Matron Thuria and believes Malis will make a good future House Elder.
Thoas (Miner) - Blacksmith; father of Dymas
Thuria (Noble, Female) - Matron of House Attis; mother of Malis and Cybele. She's concerned about rumors at their iron mine and may ask the player to investigate.

Location - Kosha
Areithous (Merchant) - Runs the Roasted Haunch, selling meats and drinks. Engaged to marry Laodice.
Atreus (Merchant) - A sly gem merchant who buys and sells gems. Has a shop but won't conduct business while at restaurants. Knows about general locations and politics in Cythera.
Ennomus (Drunk) - A drunk who offers information about the Second Tyrant's tomb for money. If given at least 40 oboloi, he reveals the clue "Two Paces North Past Noon" and provides a key before passing out.
Guards (Guard) - Kosha's guards who provide local information and directions while maintaining professional distance.
Itanos (Mage, Male) - A disorganized mage who serves as Judge of Kosha. He discusses magical lore, has a stolen Sapphire Book of Wisdom, and shares concerns about recent strange events in Kosha.
Laodice (Wench) - Cook for House Comana; engaged to marry Areithous.
Myus (Noble, Male) - The confident young leader of House Comana in Kosha. Elder of House Comana and oldest of the Comana brothers. With Ascertainment skill, player can detect he frequently lies. Particularly suspicious regarding questions about dead people and a green crystal.
Pelagon (Merchant) - Servant of House Comana and an Undine agent. Has a dark secret revealed when he transforms into a creature called Magpie when confronting the player about Crolna shards.

Location - Pnyx
Alcyone (Mage, Female) - A student mage who provides information about the Magisterium, its faculty, and magical lore including golems and underground tunnels.
Clytemnestra (Mage, Female) - A stressed female student specializing in Rune magic. Provides information about student life and faculty members.
Danae (Mage, Female) - Student studying Healing magic. Like Clytemnestra, provides information about student life and faculty.
Fountain (None) - Wishing fountain found in the catacombs beneath Pnyx. Grants one wish per visitor and provides humorous responses.
Guards (Guard) - Pnyx's guards who protect the Magisterium and direct visitors.
Helen (Peasant, Female) - Runs the Study Hall serving meals and drinks to students and mages. Member of House Dodona, wife of Paris and mother of Niobe. Shares gossip when players buy drinks.
Lindus (Mage, Male) - Headmaster who can teach Mana and Casting. Trains mages and provides magical instruction.
Lycaon (Mage, Male) - Student with undecided major who discusses the school's teachers and other students.
Menelaus (Mage, Male) - Student studying alchemy. Provides information about other students, faculty, and Pnyx's layout. Mentions rumors about hidden tunnels containing treasures or failed experiments.
Niobe (Child) - Daughter of Helen and Paris.
Palaestra (Mage, Female) - Master of Alchemy who can teach Alchemy.
Paris (Peasant, Male) - Runs a provisions shop. Member of House Dodona, husband of Helen and father of Niobe.
Peisander (Mage, Male) - Junior student with undecided major. Shares knowledge about mages, teachers, and other students.
Peleus (Mage, Male) - Student studying Runic magic. Discusses topics like alchemy, golems, and mysterious tunnels beneath Pnyx.
Pheres (Mage, Male) - Master Healer who can teach Healing. Requests a harpy egg for research and teaches healing spells to qualified students.
Protesilaus (Mage, Male) - Student studying Healing magic. Provides basic information about the school and its inhabitants.
Selinus (Mage, Male) - Master mage and Librarian. Elderly librarian who trades passwords for returned sapphire books. Knows about all books in the library.
Semele (Mage, Female) - Student and younger sister of the freemage Eioneus. Provides information about other students and mentions her brother's work with magical weapons.
Thrasymedes (Mage, Male) - Student studying Healing magic.
Tros (Mage, Male) - Master of Runic magic who can teach Runic magic.

Location - Ayrit
Sabinate (Seldane, Noble) - Elder of the Seldane; father of Jhiaxus.
Seqedher (Seldane, Female) - Watcher of the second Ayrit. Only speaks to those with sufficient karma (>40). Says the Seldane are of the Earth, not Air/Fire/Sea. References their former home of Maayti becoming a swamp.
Unhayt (Seldane, Male) - Doorkeeper of the second Ayrit.
Uset (Seldane, Female) - Herald of the second Ayrit.

Location - Flax Farm
Alcmena (Peasant, Female) - Works on the Flax farm; wife of Pelops and mother of Asteropaeus.
Asteropaeus (Child) - Son of Alcmena and Pelops. A shy young boy who won't engage in conversation.
Pelops (Peasant, Male) - Works on the Flax Farm; husband of Alcmena and father of Asteropaeus. Sells flax for 7 oboloi per bale.

Location - Iron Mine
Amphidamas (Merchant) - Foreman of House Attis' Iron Mine who reports ghost sightings that have halted work.
Erechtheus (Miner) - Miner who refuses to work due to a ghost in the mine.
Eurybates (Hunter) - Cook and hunter for the workers at the mine.
Jhiaxus (Seldane, Male) - Son of Sabinate and father of Bahoudin/Magpie. Initially speaks unknown language ("Au an Gernek ers"). After learning Seldane, reveals he's grieving for lost love Jinrai.
Lycurgus (Miner) - Miner.
Rhesus (Miner) - Miner.
Thamyris (Miner) - Miner who refuses to return to work due to a ghost in the mine.

Location - Khalkis
Guard (Guard) - Guards the swamp ruins, only allowing entry when Larisa approves visitors.
Joppa (Peasant, Male) - Assistant to Larisa. Provides information about various locations and people in Cythera, though somewhat dismissively.
Larisa (Wench) - Explorer studying Seldane ruins; sister of Timon. Works for Myus of House Comana.

Ungrouped
Borus (Peasant, Male) - Winemaker at Southland Vineyard; husband of Briseis. Concerned about Glaucus's wine going sour and offers to help.
Briseis (Peasant, Female) - Wife of Borus who handles business operations at Southland Vineyard. Received a large order from House Comana.
Charax (Mage, Male) - A nervous, unkempt Freemage studying alchemy at house north of Pnyx. Initially dismissive but becomes helpful if referred by Palaestra.
Demodocus (Hunter) - Famous travelling bard who performs at inns. Shares concerns about bandits and Alaric's declining health.
Eioneus (Miner) - Freemage weaponsmith in cave near River Styx source. Older brother of Semele. Can magically improve weapons using obsidian and lava.
Eudoxus (Brigand) - Kidnapper of Ariadne at abandoned farmhouse near Odemia.
Ghosts (Ghost) - Spirits of the mages betrayed by Tavara in Abydos. Appear as vacant-eyed figures with minimal communication.
Glaucus (Merchant) - Owner of the North Shore Vineyard. Distraught because his wine stores mysteriously turned sour overnight.
Ignae (FireSprite) - Fire spirit in cave near River Styx source. Speaks cryptically about the four elements and their balance.
Jinrai (None) - Spirit of Jhiaxus' lost love at Land's End Volcano. Appears as vision discussing truth and perception, explaining conflict between Sea and Earth people.
Meleager (Drunk) - Mercenary at Sitia Bridge who can teach Barehand and join quest for a fee. Claims to be a former personal bodyguard.
Omen (None) - Undine who communicates through visions. Found everywhere but nowhere.
Prusa (Mage, Female) - Insane freemage at River Styx source. Speaks in cryptic riddles that change based on game days.
Timon (Mage, Male) - Freemage studying Headwater Ruins; brother of Larisa. Can join quest. Studies Metics/Seldane and interested in glowing crystals.
UrSylph (Whisp) - Last surviving Air spirit imprisoned north of Great Swamp along River Styx.


The below presents all of Cythera's dialogue in a flattened form. In order to make the dialogue consistent and more computer-readable while maintaining its human readability, a markup language was designed for formatting.  The following key explains what each symbol signifies in the dialogue. It is assumed that the player is female and that the time is evening.
Prompts all appear in capital letters followed by a line break.
# - Surrounds text that appears at the bottom of the dialogue window.  This is often text seen at the start of a conversation or when someone hands you something or performs an action
[] - Surrounds trigger text.  Many prompts rely on triggers.  Any time the user has to make a selection ("Yes" or "No" or a location when asking directions), a trigger is used.  Also, any time a prompt relies on the status of the game plot (Was Ariadne rescued? Did you solve the murder? Have you found the ruffian encampment? Is Timon in your party?) a trigger is used. A single conversation branch would be surrounded by one set of brackets.  For example "[Before murder]" and "[After murder]" appear at the same level and should be considered as separate responses.  Nested triggers are supported simply by adding another layer of brackets around the trigger text.  So, "[After murder]" could branch into two additional, separate responses like "[[Before solving]]" and "[[After solving]]".  There is no requirement on trigger balance, meaning that triggers at the same level do not need the same number of children and triggers can have 0, 1, 2, or more nested triggers.  In the interactive viewer, these are parsed in real time and displayed alongside the current prompt as a drop-down selection for trigger state.
* - Precedes special prompts.  These are prompts that you either don't have to write anything to access or that entail a larger set of words you might type.  For instance, *GREETING is the greeting, and *UNKOWN is the default response.  Some characters randomly reply with a generic response (like Polydamas when asking you to speak up); these can be denoted by *RANDOM or something similar.  *PLAY is the dice playing prompt.
: - Denotes speaker change.  For back-and-forth or multi-character discussion, the name of the character should be at the start of the line, followed by a colon and then the text.  It should be read as follows:
Player: the text would appear next to your portrait
<NPC>: if the NPC's name is the same as the speaker you are talking to, text would appear next to them.  If it is another NPC, the middle section would populate with that NPC's portrait, and they speak the text.  The text would continue to appear next to the current speaker until a new name is given (either Player or another NPC's name).
{} - Contains triggers that do not affect the current text being displayed.  These are computations like the player's amount of oboloi or directions to a place from the current spot.  They are also triggers like the dialogue window closing, a text box being displayed, or a reference to another prompt being accessed.  Such triggers can be about anything, so long as they don't change the flow of conversation like a [] trigger would.  {See "..."} is a special one that should be considered as a redirect to another prompt, which is how it is handled by the interactive viewer.  Other examples:
{Directions}
{Conversation Ends}
{Game Ends}
{Enter Text}
{Display Text}
{Player's oboloi count}
% - Surrounds notes and comments.


General info known by most people:
Cythera is the name of the land you are on.
Alaric is the LandKing, who rules from his stronghold at the northern end of the mountains.
Abydos is the Lost Settlement.
Ambrosia is the food of the gods.
Cademia is the oldest city in Cythera, where River Sitia flows into the sea.
Catamarca is a resort town on a peninsula east of Cademia.
There are a number of poisonous snakes and other creatures that you need to watch out for. Fortunately, the Mages have developed an antidote potion which can save your life. There is also a folk remedy, unguent, though it's rarely effective.
Kosha is a fairly new town on the coast south of Cademia.
Mages are trained at the Magisterium in Pnyx.
Metics are a legendary race who, according to legend, used to live here.
Kosha is a fairly new town on the coast south of Cademia.
Harpies are strange dark flying creatures - quite strange and dangerous.
Milcom was the head of the former House Atussa.
Matron Thuria runs House Attis.
The Tyrants were the earliest rulers of Cythera.  It was Alaric that overthrew the Third Tyrant, ending their reign.
Gene was a great man, taken before his time.
Elder Propontis runs House Strymon.
Elder Philinus is the head of House Nicander.
Scylla are the sea monsters that are the terrors of the sea.
River Sitia flows from LandKing Hall to the sea at Cademia.
Pnyx is a city on the western coast of Cythera, home of the Magisterium.
House Strymon is the ruling House of Catamarca.
Folk remedy unguent is better for healing minor scratches than anything major.
Odemia is a rustic town along the coast north east of Cademia.
House Comana is headed by Myus.
House Nicander is the ruling House of Odemia.
House Dodona is a minor power in Catamarca.
The best musician is certainly Demodocus, the famous traveling bard.
House Attis is the former ruling House of Cademia.
House Atussa is the former ruling House of Odemia.
Elder Propontis runs House Strymon.
Elder Philinus is the head of House Nicander.
House Comana is the ruling House of Kosha.
Oboloi (singular: obol, plural: oboloi) is the currency of the land.

HISTORY
[After discussing Chrysothemis]
 My history, I remember a few parts. Living with my mother, as a child in Catamarca. When she died, I left Catamarca to roam.
 How I survived, I don't know. I think I was seeking out the exiled Mages, for I felt I had powers beyond those of normals, and I would learn of them.
 Instead of finding them, I wandered into a cave, but it was not a cave. I crossed a bridge of stars, and found an abandoned hall or temple of some sort.
 Who built this place, I knew not. It was empty and abandoned, but great power called to me.
 I remember standing on the edge of the Abyss, and felt myself surrender to it, becoming one with it, with the Land.
 It was then that I became the LandKing. I know not how long I stood there - for years, it was certain. And in the end, I had completed the bond.
 I knew then that the rule of the Tyrants must be ended. And thus I ended it - and from that came the current system of Houses and Judges.
 All that seems to be failing now - perhaps my time has ended...
[Before discussing Chrysothemis]
 There are days I don't remember what happened the day before, much less more ancient history.
 For some reason, though, the number 201 seems important. If you find out why, please tell me...

JOURNEY
We are not from this place, but from the same Earth that you are. We lived on an island called Thera, until Enesidaone reclaimed it.
But that was not without warning, for a stranger, in the form of the Holy Bull appeared, telling us to flee. We saw Thera destroyed from the safety of our ships at sea.
That night a storm such as has never been before tossed us about, until in the morning when we woke up, tossed aside at the beach near what would become the city of Cademia.
Or so goes the legend. I am not a historian - perhaps you should ask another...

 [[You have oboloi]]
   {See "*Play"}
[Yes]
 It's very simple - we've got three dice. My two are black and your one is white. I throw out the first one, and then you throw your one die. If they match, you win 2 to 1.
 If they don't match, I roll my second die. If your white die is 'trapped' between my two black dice, I win. By trapped, I mean if the number on your die is between my two - this includes equalling my second die.
 For every point higher or lower than my highest or lowest die is, that's how much you win. For example, if I roll a 5 and a 6, then if you had rolled a 1, you'd get 4 to 1!
 We have a special rule - if your payback were 1 to 1, it just means we automatically play again = no need to redo your bet or anything.
 Sound like fun?
 [[Yes]]

STORY
[Initial]
 I already told Thuria, but I suppose I could tell it one more time - I don't much like thinking about it.
 Anyway, I was working deep, extending the tunnels. It's hard work, but I like it better than shuffling through dirt.
 So, as I was saying, I'm in the deep back part of the tunnels, and suddenly I feel the earth start to move.
 Nothing worse than a cave-in, so I run back to the older tunnels as fast as I can, dodging some large rocks as I go.
 But then one of them hit me on the head, and I'm out - don't know how long, but my torch burned out.
 I had a strange dream, too - something about sorrow and loss, only more intense than a man should ever feel.
 Anyway, I come to, and manage to strike up one of my spare torches. Always carry spare torches that you hope you never need.
 I look, expecting to be trapped, but no, just some large rocks disturbed in the new tunnel.
 Not that you can always tell, mind you, since a rough tunnel can look like it's been collapsed, and that's normal.
 Anyway, I looked to see how much work I lost - and turns out not only was it in pretty good shape, but there's this opening.
 This is pretty good normally, since a small cave pocket can save weeks of digging.
 So just when I think my luck has changed - not only did I survive the cave-in, but I'm ahead. I climb down the opening.
 And there I saw it. All sort of white and glowing in the torchlight, sitting on a rock.
 Never seen anything like it - it just turns to me, with its dead eyes, and says something I don't understand.
 Didn't know that the dead spoke a different language, but there's lots I don't know.
 This was too much for me - seeing the agony on its face. A tortured soul, it was, I know now.
 I nearly dropped my torch fleeing - it was too much for me.
 #He buries his head in his hands and sobs a bit, his shoulders shaking.#
 Please - give me a minute to pull myself together...
 {Conversation Ends}

      Berossus: Hm, very interesting. Do go on.
      Naxos: Your Honor, with all due respect, but he is but a child. Can you really trust his testimony? He's a Ratcatcher sewer-rat!

      Naxos: Yes, your Honor. I am sorry.
      Berossus: Please continue.
      Dryas: Well, I was on my way to visit Halos on Guild business, when I saw Cybele enter his shop - she didn't see me.

[No]
 Well, at one time is was common for a mage to construct a golem - a being made of clay and magically empowered.
 They were used to perform simple physical tasks, being immensely strong.  But there was a problem.
 There was no way to unmake the golem, save physically destroying them.  And when their master died, nobody could control them.
 They would run amuck, causing great violence and destruction.  existence today.
 The beginning student learns that all their spells can have long lasting consequences because of this.

[No]

 [[You have no oboloi]]

 [[You have oboloi]]
   {See "*Play"}
[Yes]
 It's very simple - we've got three dice. My two are black and your one is white. I throw out the first one, and then you throw your one die. If they match, you win 2 to 1.
 If they don't match, I roll my second die. If your white die is 'trapped' between my two black dice, I win. By trapped, I mean if the number on your die is between my two - this includes equalling my second die.
 For every point higher or lower than my highest or lowest die is, that's how much you win. For example, if I roll a 5 and a 6, then if you had rolled a 1, you'd get 4 to 1!
 We have a special rule - if your payback were 1 to 1, it just means we automatically play again = no need to redo your bet or anything.
 Sound like fun?

Jinrai
*GREETING
#A vision appears before you.#
You have honored our memory. You have fed our KA. In return, we shall tell you of truth.
Truth is one's perception of the facts. The facts are singular, but truth is manyfold. One can be told facts. but one must make truth.
Jhiaxus and we shared the truth, and neither of our people would accept that. The mixing of Sea and Earth was heresy to them.
Ours were the people of the Sea, Jhiaxus' were the people of the Earth. Our conflict was eternal.
Only here, where the powers of Fire were strong could we hide, but alas, our people found us, destroyed us.
Consider the facts, young mortal, and be set free by the truth. The ruins that were built by the Seldane - that is fact.
The ruins that were destroyed - that is fact. Ask yourself how, ask yourself why, ask yourself who.
And for what purpose was this done? With what tools was this done? And how are those tools used?
A tool is neither good nor evil - it is but a tool. It is in the hands of the wielder that a tool's purpose is determined.
You must learn who your enemies are, and who are your allies, but in the end, you can only trust yourself, for all others are blinded by their truth.
There is one you can trust, who is our offspring. But in the end, do not be blinded by a truth which is not yours.
This, of course, is our truth. Your truth, you must make.
#The vision seems to fade, and you awaken, as if from a dream.#
{Conversation Ends}

Omen
*GREETING
[Before Omen's Test]
 #A strange looking face suddenly fades into view before you..#
 Poor little human - so far from home, and so far from the truth...
 You do know, don't you, that Alaric is not all he says he is. True, he is bound to the land, but as a usurper.
 He does not belong in the position of power, and he upsets the balance of the world by being there.
 And you, my friend, are just another puppet of his. Now is the time of change, and you are at the pivot point.
 But which way will the balance swing? To the East? Or to the West?
 And myself? I am Omen, and my master has instructed me to watch for one like you.
 At great price to myself I come to you, as a vision, to warn and guide you, protect and teach you.
 Know you this - there is more going on here than meets the eye, a power struggle as old as the world itself.
 And in this struggle, you are trapped. If you ever expect to be free, heed my advice well.
 Alaric must be destroyed to restore the land, and in the end, you will do this.
 To aid you in your destiny, we have a small gift, to prove our word.
 But first, you must learn the ways of the land, and to this end my master has prepared a small test of your abilities.
 For you must hone your skills. As a sword is tempered, so must you be.
 Seek your way out of this proving grounds, and along the way you might find your reward.
 Follow our counsel, and you will be well rewarded - ignore it at your own peril.
 Until we meet again, be on your guard, and trust not what your eyes behold.
 {Conversation Ends}
[After Omen's Test]
 #The vision of Omen returns..#
 Well done, human. That wasn't so hard now, was it.
 [[You didn't pick up the ring]]
   Too bad you didn't find your reward - your lack of attentiveness does not bode well...
   Still, I am not without pity - I promised you help, and so you shall have it.
   I shall grant you another boon - this magical mapping device, that lets you see where you've been.
   Remember our advice, and perhaps we shall meet again - I shall be watching you and your actions.
   If you ever find yourself returning to my little 'Test', I'll tell you a secret.
   When you pull the lever in the first room, an illusionary wall opens under the torch, so you can walk through the wall there.
   From that secret room, just take the ladder down to my quarters here.
   In the mean time, I will return you to your quarters - speak to no one of this encounter of ours.
   {Conversation Ends}

[Entering Cademia the first time]
 #The vision of Omen returns before you..#
 Cademia. Mother City. Once you've lived in Cademia, no other place can be home.
 Keep your wits here - there are various miscreants that roam the back alleys, especially at night.
 Much lies buried beneath its ancient buildings - the city has much history that is now lost.
 #Omen fades away...#

[Entering Abydos the first time]
 #The vision of Omen returns before you..#
 Lost Abydos - now just a reminder of a great failure...
 None of the Mages dare admit it, but Abydos was destroyed by their leader.
 The Mage Tavara destroyed the city when the people rejected his leadership.
 If you enter the tunnels below the city, you'll even find his workroom - but be careful.
 That place is guarded by a fearsome magical being that you'd call a daemon.
 Don't say you weren't warned.
 #Omen fades away..#

 #An anxious vision of Omen suddenly appears.#
 No!!!! You've ruined it all!!! My master!!! You've destroyed my master!!!
 Filthy human, think you're so clever, spoil our plans, and destroy my master.
 How you were able to detect the essence of my master hidden in the Crolna, I don't know.
 But know you this - you've seen through the shadows of my master's plans, for we have underestimated you.
 We won't make that mistake again. You've not seen the last of us.
 Our battle with the forces of Earth continue, and we will be victorious.
 The seeds of the destruction of that abomination Alaric have been sown, and with his destruction, so must Earth fall.
 Our agents are everywhere, appearing as you do, and there is no way for you to know who is true and who is false.
 This day's battle might go to you, but we are the Sea, and time is ours to command.
 We will endure, we will sow chaos and dissent among your people, and we will destroy you all.
 #Omen disappears, but somehow you doubt this is the last of him..#

[No]
 Well, at one time is was common for a mage to construct a golem - a being made of clay and magically empowered.
 They were used to perform simple physical tasks, being immensely strong.  But there was a problem.
 There was no way to unmake the golem, save physically destroying them.  And when their master died, nobody could control them.
 They would run amuck, causing great violence and destruction.  existence today.
 The beginning student learns that all their spells can have long lasting consequences because of this.

[No]

 [[You have oboloi]]
   {See "*Play"}
[Yes]
 It's very simple - we've got three dice. My two are black and your one is white. I throw out the first one, and then you throw your one die. If they match, you win 2 to 1.
 If they don't match, I roll my second die. If your white die is 'trapped' between my two black dice, I win. By trapped, I mean if the number on your die is between my two - this includes equalling my second die.
 For every point higher or lower than my highest or lowest die is, that's how much you win. For example, if I roll a 5 and a 6, then if you had rolled a 1, you'd get 4 to 1!
 We have a special rule - if your payback were 1 to 1, it just means we automatically play again = no need to redo your bet or anything.
 Sound like fun?
